//! Business-state regressions for declarative day-two adoption.
use super::*;
use crate::{Context,Engine,WriteState,wire};
use crate::engine::release::ReleaseActivation;
use serde_json::{json,Value as Json};
fn compile(source:&str)->(Schema,Vec<crate::ir::Program>){
    let b=snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source.into())]);
    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",b.diagnostics);
    (serde_json::from_str(&serde_json::to_string(&b.schema).unwrap()).unwrap(),b.programs.into_iter().map(|p|serde_json::from_str(&serde_json::to_string(&p.program).unwrap()).unwrap()).collect())
}
fn engine(source:&str)->Engine{let(s,p)=compile(source);Engine::new_release(Store::memory().unwrap(),s,p,1,None).unwrap()}
fn adopt(e:&mut Engine,source:&str,n:u64)->Result<u64,RuntimeError>{
    let(s,p)=compile(source);let previous=e.store().meta("release").unwrap().map(|s|s.parse().unwrap());
    e.adopt_release(s,p,&ReleaseActivation::new(n,previous),false)
}
fn seed(e:&mut Engine,table:&str,row:Json){let row=wire::row_from_json(&e.schema().tables[table],&row).unwrap();e.seed([(table.into(),row)]).unwrap();}
fn row(e:&mut Engine,table:&str,id:&str)->Value{e.store_mut().transaction().unwrap().get(table,id).unwrap().unwrap()}
fn field(e:&mut Engine,table:&str,id:&str,c:&str)->Value{row(e,table,id).field(c).unwrap().clone()}
const BASE:&str="table bookings:\n  weight: int\n  length: int\n  public 'fixture'\n  insert <- allow\n  update <- allow\n  delete <- allow\n  online only\nmutation edit():\n  update bookings[id = 'a'] {weight:4000}\nmutation remove():\n  delete bookings[id = 'a']\n";
fn ferry()->String{BASE.replace("  weight: int","  vehicleClass: enum('car', 'van', 'commercial')\n  weight: int")+"backfill bookings vehicleClass(weight: int, length: int):\n  {vehicleClass: 'commercial' if weight > 3500 else 'van' if length >= 5 else 'car'}\nmutation classify():\n  update bookings[id = 'a'] {vehicleClass:'van'}\n"}
fn write(e:&mut Engine,op:&str){let r=e.mutate(op,None,Context::new("alice",Default::default())).unwrap();assert!(matches!(r.state,WriteState::Sent{..}),"{r:?}");}
#[test]
fn conditional_backfill_rollback_older_writes_and_forward_catchup(){
 let mut e=engine(BASE);seed(&mut e,"bookings",json!({"id":"a","weight":1000,"length":4}));
 seed(&mut e,"bookings",json!({"id":"b","weight":1000,"length":5}));
 seed(&mut e,"bookings",json!({"id":"c","weight":4000,"length":4}));
 adopt(&mut e,&ferry(),2).unwrap();
 for(id,class)in[("a","car"),("b","van"),("c","commercial")]{assert_eq!(field(&mut e,"bookings",id,"vehicleClass"),Value::String(class.into()));}
 assert!(e.store().adoption_report.backfills.iter().any(|s|s.contains("transformed 3 rows")));
 let g=e.generation();adopt(&mut e,&ferry(),2).unwrap();assert_eq!(g,e.generation());
 write(&mut e,"classify");
 adopt(&mut e,BASE,1).unwrap();assert!(row(&mut e,"bookings","a").field("vehicleClass").is_err());
 seed(&mut e,"bookings",json!({"id":"d","weight":3600,"length":1}));
 adopt(&mut e,&ferry(),2).unwrap();assert_eq!(field(&mut e,"bookings","a","vehicleClass"),Value::String("van".into()));
 assert_eq!(field(&mut e,"bookings","d","vehicleClass"),Value::String("commercial".into()));
 adopt(&mut e,BASE,1).unwrap();write(&mut e,"edit");
 let before=e.generation();let error=adopt(&mut e,&ferry(),2).unwrap_err().to_string();assert!(error.contains("edited")&&error.contains("historical inputs changed"),"{error}");assert_eq!(before,e.generation());
}
#[test]
fn older_input_change_recomputes_unedited_derived_value(){
 let mut e=engine(BASE);seed(&mut e,"bookings",json!({"id":"a","weight":1000,"length":4}));
 adopt(&mut e,&ferry(),2).unwrap();adopt(&mut e,BASE,1).unwrap();write(&mut e,"edit");
 adopt(&mut e,&ferry(),2).unwrap();assert_eq!(field(&mut e,"bookings","a","vehicleClass"),Value::String("commercial".into()));
}
const CUE:&str="table cues:\n  status: enum('standby','done')\n  public 'fixture'\n  update <- allow\n  online only\n";
fn cues()->String{CUE.replace("enum('standby','done')","enum('standby','complete','skipped')")+"backfill cues rename(status: enum('standby','done')):\n  {status: 'complete' if status = 'done' else status}\nmutation skip():\n  update cues['a'] {status:'skipped'}\n"}
#[test]
fn enum_rename_reverses_exact_preimage_and_refuses_post_backfill_edits(){

 assert_eq!(field(&mut e,"cues","a","status"),Value::String("complete".into()));
 adopt(&mut e,CUE,1).unwrap();assert_eq!(field(&mut e,"cues","a","status"),Value::String("done".into()));
 adopt(&mut e,&cues(),2).unwrap();write(&mut e,"skip");let g=e.generation();
 let error=adopt(&mut e,CUE,1).unwrap_err().to_string();assert!(error.contains("changed after backfill"),"{error}");assert_eq!(g,e.generation());
 assert_eq!(field(&mut e,"cues","a","status"),Value::String("skipped".into()));
}

The refresh uses the exact active declaration key and runs in the row’s transaction. I’m checking whether it also preserves custody when one declaration consumes another’s output or a retained reference is retargeted; the four new tests cover only scalar cases and rename absence.

    fn scan_keys(&self, table: &str, index_name: &str, lo: &[u8], hi: &[u8], dir: Dir, limit: usize) -> Result<Vec<(Value, Vec<u8>)>, RuntimeError> {
        let t = self.num(table)?;
        let ti = self.num(&format!("{table}.{index_name}"))?;
        let mut statement = self.tx.prepare_cached(scan_sql(dir))?;
        let mut rows = statement.query(params![t, ti, lo, hi, limit as i64])?;
        let mut out = Vec::new();
        while let Some(row) = rows.next()? {
            let data = row.get_ref(0)?.as_str().map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?;
            out.push((serde_json::from_str(data).map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?, row.get(1)?,
            ));
        }
        Ok(out)
    }

    /// How many entries sit under `prefix`, counting at most `limit`.
    pub fn count_prefix(&self, table: &str, index_name: &str, prefix: &[Value], limit: u64) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        let ti = self.num(&format!("{table}.{index_name}"))?;
        let base = key::encode(prefix);
        let hi = key::upper_bound(&base);
        let count: i64 = self.tx.prepare_cached("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM (SELECT 1 FROM i WHERE ti = ?1 AND k >= ?2 AND k < ?3 LIMIT ?4)")?.query_row(params![ti, base, hi, limit as i64], |row| row.get(0))?;
        Ok(count as u64)
    }

    /// Write a row image: a fresh insert when `old` is none, else a replace
    /// that touches only the index entries whose keys changed (a `words`
    /// index has one per token). Returns the number of entries written.
    pub fn put(&self, table_name: &str, table: &Table, row: &Value, old: Option<&Value>) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        self.put_indexed(table_name, table, row, old, "INSERT INTO i(ti, k, id) VALUES (?1, ?2, ?3)")
    }

    /// A replacement loader has already cleared the rows and created its
    /// temporary index heap. It must install those indexes before any read.
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.4 — native replacement sorts complete indexes inside the publication transaction.
    pub fn put_loading_indexes(&self, table_name: &str, table: &Table, row: &Value) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        self.put_indexed(table_name, table, row, None, "INSERT INTO temp._device_index_load(ti, k, id) VALUES (?1, ?2, ?3)")
    }

    fn put_indexed(&self, table_name: &str, table: &Table, row: &Value, old: Option<&Value>, insert_index: &str) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        self.clear_activation_scan();
        if table.ephemeral.is_some() { return Err(RuntimeError::Store("durable transaction cannot write an ephemeral table".into())); }
        let t = self.num(table_name)?;
        let id = row.field("id")?.as_str()?;
        narrowed::check_write(self,table_name,table,row,old)?;
        backfills::observe_write(self,t,id,table,row,old)?;
        let keys = crate::rows::index_write(table_name, table, row, old, id)?;
        self.changes.borrow_mut().record(table_name, table, old, Some(row));
        let data = canonical_json(row);
        let sql = if old.is_some() { "UPDATE r SET data = ?3 WHERE t = ?1 AND id = ?2" } else { "INSERT INTO r(t, id, data) VALUES (?1, ?2, ?3)" };
        self.tx.prepare_cached(sql)?.execute(params![t, id, data])?;
        let mut insert = self.tx.prepare_cached(insert_index)?;
        let mut entries = 0;
        for crate::rows::IndexWrite { name, before, after } in keys {
            if before == after { continue; }
            let ti = self.num(&format!("{table_name}.{name}"))?;
            for key in before.iter().filter(|key| !after.contains(key)) {
                self.conn().prepare_cached("DELETE FROM i WHERE ti = ?1 AND k = ?2")?.execute(params![ti, key])?;
                entries += 1;
            }
            for key in after.iter().filter(|key| !before.contains(key)) {
                insert.execute(params![ti, key, id])?;
                entries += 1;
            }
        }
        entries += self.update_cap_counts(table_name, table, old, Some(row))?;
        Ok(entries)
    }

    pub fn delete(&self, table_name: &str, table: &Table, old: &Value) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        self.clear_activation_scan();
        if table.ephemeral.is_some() { return Err(RuntimeError::Store("durable transaction cannot write an ephemeral table".into())); }
        let t = self.num(table_name)?;
        self.changes.borrow_mut().record(table_name, table, Some(old), None);
        let id = old.field("id")?.as_str()?.to_owned();
        dormant::delete(self,t,&id,table_name)?;
        self.conn().prepare_cached("DELETE FROM r WHERE t = ?1 AND id = ?2")?.execute(params![t, id])?;

# Rules

**Snapback 2 optimizes the edit-to-verified loop — the agent's building an app on it, and ours building it.**

Speed is the constraint. Everything below exists to keep the edit-to-verified loop short
and to stop this repository accreting apparatus faster than anyone can remove it. These
rules bind `snapback2/` and the LLPs numbered 1000 and up; Snapback 1 is baselined, not
exempt, and its numbers may only fall.

Each rule is **[check]** (enforced by `caps`) or **[review]** (enforced by a human).

## Budgets — every budget is a trade, not a limit

- **5 blocking checks, 60s total.** A sixth requires deleting one, in the same PR. **[check]**
- **59 documents in the working set** (`llp/current/`). Today's count. A 60th requires
  archiving one — which is just removing the link. Snapback 2 aims to leave this at 15;
  Snapback 1's links are archived at promotion. **[check]**
- **12 documents in the foundation** (`llp/foundation/`). **[check]**
- **This file: 700 words.** If it grows, something becomes a check or stops being a rule. **[check]**
- **1,500 lines per source file, baseline 204.** Generated files exempt — and generated
  files are built, not committed; so are benchmark run records under `benchmarks/runs/`,
  which are evidence, not source. The baseline is Snapback 1's oversize count on the day
  the rule landed; it may not grow, and `snapback2/` contributes zero to it. **[check]**

## Blocking checks

`caps` · `ref-check` · `snapback2/scripts/test.sh` · `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs --check` · `check-inventories`

## Loop shape

- **Every check reports all failures in one run.** Fail-fast is banned. **[check]**
- **Nothing blocks on anything slower than 60 seconds.** Slow verification runs on a
  schedule, attributed to the breaking commit, and files a bounded set of issues. **[review]**
- **Fix loops get 3 rounds.** Then stop: escalate to a human or descope. **[review]**

## Time budgets

Tracked every run of `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs` (appends `snapback2/metrics/loop.jsonl`).
A regression is a P0 with a name on it.

| | budget |
|---|---|
| Incremental rebuild after a one-line edit | 15s |
| Test what you just changed | 30s |
| Blocking gate suite | 60s |
| Full build, warm cache | 5 min |
| **`snapback2 check` on a program (the agent's product latency)** | **1s** |
| **`snapback2 doctor` boot-to-ready** | **0.5s** |

## Scope

- **`NOT-DOING.md` is binding.** Moving something onto the doing-list means writing why
  and taking something off. **[review]**
- **Delete; don't deprecate.** No compat shims or migration paths before promotion. **[review]**
- **Nothing is compiled, transpiled, or generated at runtime that could be built ahead of
  time.** **[review]**
- **A spec needs an implementer and a date, or it isn't written.** **[review]**
- **Only implemented specs bind.** A document with no running code behind it is a
  proposal, and a proposal cannot block a PR or justify a check. **[review]**

## Agents

- **Agents remove apparatus freely and add none.** An agent PR that adds a check, script,
  registry, config file, or design doc needs explicit human approval saying so. **[review]**
- **2026-09-06 session exception:** the user delegated the search implementation
  decisions; one `snapback2-core/build.rs` may generate pinned Unicode tables at
  build time, alongside the existing package build. No new gate, registry,
  runtime generator, or Unicode dependency is authorized by this exception. **[review]**
- **Snapback 2 LLPs are numbered 1000 and up**; `llp-create` must be told. **[review]**
- **Snapback 4 LLPs are numbered 3000 and up** (`snapback4/`, LLP 3000); it adopts these budgets
  and loop shape as its own and keeps its scope in `snapback4/NOT-DOING.md`. **[review]**

## Shared machines

- Warm, named lanes, one per stream of work. Never clone fresh; reuse the worktree.
- **Never `git stash`.** Use a temporary commit.
- Kill only PIDs you recorded at launch. Never by name or pattern.

#[test]
fn reentry_keeps_absence_distinct_from_a_later_default() {
 let old="table items:\n  public 'fixture'\n  online only\n";
 let next=old.replace("  public","  n: int default 100\n  public")+"backfill items one(n: int ?):\n  {n:(n ?? 0) + 1}\n";
 let mut e=engine(old);seed(&mut e,"items",json!({"id":"a"}));adopt(&mut e,&next,2).unwrap();assert_eq!(field(&mut e,"items","a","n"),Value::Int(1));
 adopt(&mut e,"query empty():\n  return true\n",3).unwrap();adopt(&mut e,&next,2).unwrap();assert_eq!(field(&mut e,"items","a","n"),Value::Int(1));
 adopt(&mut e,old,1).unwrap();adopt(&mut e,&next,2).unwrap();assert_eq!(field(&mut e,"items","a","n"),Value::Int(1));
}
#[test]
fn saved_reference_inputs_keep_their_own_lifetime_after_live_retargeting() {
 for kind in ["parents","id"] {
 let parents="table parents:\n  n: int\n  public 'fixture'\n  online only\n";
 let mutation="mutation retarget():\n  update children['c'] {parent:'q'}\n";
 let old=parents.to_owned()+"table children:\n  parent: parents on delete keep\n  public 'fixture'\n  update <- allow\n  online only\n"+mutation;
 let fill=format!("backfill children copy(parent: {kind}):\n  {{copied:(parents[parent] ! else NOT_FOUND).n}}\n");
 let next=old.replace("  parent: parents","  copied: int\n  parent: parents")+&fill;
 let dropped=next.replace("  parent: parents on delete keep\n","").replace(mutation,"");
 let mut e=engine(&old);for(id,n)in[("p",3),("q",9)]{seed(&mut e,"parents",json!({"id":id,"n":n}));}seed(&mut e,"children",json!({"id":"c","parent":"p"}));adopt(&mut e,&next,2).unwrap();write(&mut e,"retarget");adopt(&mut e,&dropped,3).unwrap();
 let table=e.schema().tables["parents"].clone();{let tx=e.store_mut().transaction().unwrap();let r=tx.get("parents","p").unwrap().unwrap();tx.delete("parents",&table,&r).unwrap();tx.commit().unwrap();}seed(&mut e,"parents",json!({"id":"p","n":77}));
 adopt(&mut e,parents,4).unwrap();let error=adopt(&mut e,&dropped,3).unwrap_err().to_string();assert!(error.contains("parent lifetime"),"{error}");
}
}

#[test]
fn a_new_reference_output_keeps_its_parent_lifetime_when_the_row_narrows() {
 let old="table parents:\n  name: text <=10\n  unique name\n  public 'fixture'\n  online only\ntable children:\n  n: int 0..10\n  public 'fixture'\n  online only\n";
 let next=old.replace("  n: int 0..10","  link: parents\n  n: int 0..5")+"backfill children link():\n  {link:(parents[name = 'p'] ! else NOT_FOUND).id}\n";
 let mut e=engine(old);seed(&mut e,"parents",json!({"id":"p","name":"p"}));seed(&mut e,"children",json!({"id":"c","n":9}));adopt(&mut e,&next,2).unwrap();assert_eq!(e.store().count("children").unwrap(),0);
 let table=e.schema().tables["parents"].clone();{let tx=e.store_mut().transaction().unwrap();let r=tx.get("parents","p").unwrap().unwrap();tx.delete("parents",&table,&r).unwrap();tx.commit().unwrap();}seed(&mut e,"parents",json!({"id":"p","name":"p"}));
 adopt(&mut e,&next.replace("int 0..5","int 0..10"),3).unwrap();assert_eq!(e.store().count("children").unwrap(),0,"a retained output reference cannot recapture a reused parent");
}
#[test]
fn a_transformed_restored_row_keeps_its_new_cells_when_it_becomes_dormant_again() {
 let old="table parents:\n  n: int 0..10\n  public 'fixture'\n  online only\ntable children:\n  label: enum('a','b')\n  n: int\n  public 'fixture'\n  online only\n";
 let narrow=old.replace("enum('a','b')","enum('a')");
 let next=old.replace("  n: int 0..10","  n: int 0..5").replace("  label:","  link: parents\n  label:")+"backfill children migration(n: int):\n  {n:n * 2, link:(parents['p'] ! else NOT_FOUND).id}\n";
 let mut e=engine(old);seed(&mut e,"parents",json!({"id":"p","n":9}));seed(&mut e,"children",json!({"id":"c","label":"b","n":3}));adopt(&mut e,&narrow,2).unwrap();adopt(&mut e,&next,3).unwrap();assert_eq!(e.store().count("children").unwrap(),0);
 adopt(&mut e,&next.replace("int 0..5","int 0..10"),4).unwrap();assert_eq!(field(&mut e,"children","c","n"),Value::Int(6));
}

  }
 }

#[test]
fn physically_sparse_defaults_are_interpreted_and_receipts_keep_that_interpretation() {
 for kind in ["int","int ?"] {
  let old="table items:\n  public 'fixture'\n  online only\n";
  let defaulted=old.replace("  public","  n: int default 3\n  public");
  let fill=format!("backfill items twice(n: {kind}):\n  {{doubled:(n ?? 0) * 2}}\n");
  let next=defaulted.replace("  n:","  doubled: int\n  n:")+&fill;
  let mut e=engine(old);seed(&mut e,"items",json!({"id":"a"}));adopt(&mut e,&defaulted,2).unwrap();
  assert!(row(&mut e,"items","a").field("n").is_err(),"fixture is physically sparse");
  adopt(&mut e,&next,3).unwrap();assert_eq!(field(&mut e,"items","a","doubled"),Value::Int(6));
  let later=next.replace("default 3","default 100");adopt(&mut e,&later,4).unwrap();adopt(&mut e,"query empty():\n  return true\n",5).unwrap();adopt(&mut e,&later,4).unwrap();
  assert_eq!(field(&mut e,"items","a","doubled"),Value::Int(6));
  let rollback=later.replace("  doubled: int\n","").replace(&fill,"");adopt(&mut e,&rollback,6).unwrap();adopt(&mut e,&later,4).unwrap();
  assert_eq!(field(&mut e,"items","a","doubled"),Value::Int(6),"rollback/forward keeps the original omitted-input interpretation");
 }
}

#[test]
fn saved_generic_id_binding_survives_type_erasure_and_multiple_restorations() {
 let parents="table parents:\n  n: int\n  public 'fixture'\n  online only\n";
 let old=parents.to_owned()+"table children:\n  parent: parents on delete keep\n  public 'fixture'\n  online only\n";
 let next=old.replace("  parent:","  copied: int\n  parent:")+"backfill children copy(parent: id):\n  {copied:(parents[parent] ! else NOT_FOUND).n}\n";
 let erased=next.replace("parent: parents on delete keep","parent: id");
 let mut e=engine(&old);seed(&mut e,"parents",json!({"id":"p","n":3}));seed(&mut e,"children",json!({"id":"c","parent":"p"}));adopt(&mut e,&next,2).unwrap();adopt(&mut e,&erased,3).unwrap();
 adopt(&mut e,parents,4).unwrap();adopt(&mut e,&erased,3).unwrap();adopt(&mut e,parents,4).unwrap();
 let table=e.schema().tables["parents"].clone();{let tx=e.store_mut().transaction().unwrap();let r=tx.get("parents","p").unwrap().unwrap();tx.delete("parents",&table,&r).unwrap();tx.commit().unwrap();}
 seed(&mut e,"parents",json!({"id":"p","n":77}));let error=adopt(&mut e,&erased,3).unwrap_err().to_string();assert!(error.contains("parent lifetime"),"{error}");
}

#[test]
fn reentering_omission_cohorts_do_not_change_a_new_declarations_source_snapshot() {
 let old="table items:\n  public 'fixture'\n  online only\n";
 let defaulted=old.replace("  public","  n: int default 3\n  public");
 let fill="backfill items twice(n: int):\n  {doubled:n * 2}\n";
 let next=defaulted.replace("  n:","  doubled: int\n  n:")+fill;
 let mut e=engine(old);seed(&mut e,"items",json!({"id":"a"}));adopt(&mut e,&defaulted,2).unwrap();adopt(&mut e,&next,3).unwrap();
 let rollback=defaulted.replace("default 3","default 100");adopt(&mut e,&rollback,4).unwrap();
 let fresh=next.replace("default 3","default 100").replace("  doubled:","  fresh: int\n  doubled:")+"backfill items current(n: int):\n  {fresh:n * 2}\n";
 adopt(&mut e,&fresh,5).unwrap();assert_eq!(field(&mut e,"items","a","doubled"),Value::Int(6));assert_eq!(field(&mut e,"items","a","fresh"),Value::Int(200));
}

#[test]
fn set_null_expiry_updates_a_column_archived_during_that_release() {
 let parent="table parents:\n  until: time\n  expire at .until\n  public 'fixture'\n  online only\n";
 let child="table children:\n  parent: parents ? on delete set null\n  cap 4 by parent\n  public 'fixture'\n  online only\n";
 let item="table items:\n  n: int\n  public 'fixture'\n  online only\n";
 let old=format!("{parent}{child}{item}");let next=old.replace("  parent: parents ? on delete set null\n","").replace("  cap 4 by parent\n","").replace("  n:","  doubled: int\n  n:")+"backfill items twice(n: int):\n  {doubled:n * 2}\n";
 let(s,p)=compile(&old);let mut e=Engine::new_release_prepared_at(Store::memory().unwrap(),s,p,&ReleaseActivation::new(1,None),false,0).unwrap();
 let rows=[("parents",json!({"id":"p","until":50})),("children",json!({"id":"c","parent":"p"})),("items",json!({"id":"i","n":3}))];
 let rows=rows.into_iter().map(|(t,r)|(t.to_owned(),wire::row_from_json(&e.schema().tables[t],&r).unwrap())).collect::<Vec<_>>();e.seed_at(rows,0).unwrap();
 let(s,p)=compile(&next);e.adopt_release_at(s,p,&ReleaseActivation::new(2,Some(1)),false,100).unwrap();assert_eq!(e.store().count("parents").unwrap(),0);
 let fresh=wire::row_from_json(&e.schema().tables["parents"],&json!({"id":"p","until":500})).unwrap();e.seed_at([("parents".into(),fresh)],100).unwrap();
 let(s,p)=compile(&old);e.adopt_release_at(s,p,&ReleaseActivation::new(1,Some(2)),false,101).unwrap();assert_eq!(field(&mut e,"children","c","parent"),Value::Null);
}

#[test]
fn inactive_generic_id_receipts_refuse_a_reused_parent_before_any_lookup() {
 let parents="table parents:\n  n: int\n  public 'fixture'\n  online only\n";
 let old=parents.to_owned()+"table children:\n  parent: parents on delete keep\n  public 'fixture'\n  online only\n";
 let fill="backfill children copy(parent: id):\n  {copied:(parents[parent] ! else NOT_FOUND).n}\n";
 let next=old.replace("  parent:","  copied: int\n  parent:")+fill;let erased=next.replace("parent: parents on delete keep","parent: id");
 let without=erased.replace(fill,"").replace("  copied: int\n","");
) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    tx.conn().execute(
        "DELETE FROM _dormant_refs WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND column_name=?3",
        params![t, id, column],
    )?;
    capture_refs_preserving(tx,t,id,column,kind,value,&BTreeSet::new())
}

pub(super) fn capture_refs_preserving(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str,column:&str,kind:&ColumnType,value:&Value,known:&BTreeSet<String>)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
    let mut refs=Vec::new();references(kind,value,&mut refs);
    for(parent,parent_id)in refs {if !known.contains(&parent_id){add_reference(tx,t,id,column,&parent,&parent_id)?;}}
    Ok(())
}

fn add_reference(
    tx: &Tx<'_>,
    t: i64,
    id: &str,
    column: &str,
    parent: &str,
    parent_id: &str,
) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    let parent_t = tx.num(parent)?;
    let invalid = !tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM r WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2)",params![parent_t,parent_id],|r|r.get::<_,bool>(0))?
        && !tx.conn().query_row(
            "SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_rows WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2) OR EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_narrow_rows WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2)",
            params![parent_t, parent_id],
            |r| r.get::<_, bool>(0),
        )?;
    tx.conn().execute(
        "INSERT OR IGNORE INTO _dormant_refs VALUES(?1,?2,?3,?4,?5,?6)",
        params![t, id, column, parent_t, parent_id, if invalid {2} else {0}],
    )?;
    Ok(())
}
pub(super) fn capture_through_parent(
    tx: &Tx<'_>,
    name: &str,
    table: &Table,
    id: &str,
    column: &str,
    row: &Value,
) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    if let Some(through) = &table.through {
        if (through.fields.contains_key(column)
            || matches!(
                column,
                crate::schema::through::PARENT_DEADLINE | crate::schema::through::DEADLINE
            ))
            && let Ok(Value::Id(parent_id)) = row.field(&through.reference)
        {
            add_reference(tx, tx.num(name)?, id, column, &through.parent, parent_id)?;
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}
            } else if kind.is_optional() {
                fields.insert(name.clone(), Value::Null);
            } else if let Some(ColumnDefault::Literal(value)) = table.defaults.get(name) {
                fields.insert(name.clone(), value.clone());
            }
        }
    }
}

/// Borrow complete images; materialize old images only when needed.
pub fn row_image<'a>(row: &'a Value, table: &Table) -> std::borrow::Cow<'a, Value> {
    if table.columns.keys().all(|column| row.field(column).is_ok()) { return std::borrow::Cow::Borrowed(row); }
    let mut image = row.clone();
    materialize_optional_fields(&mut image, table);
    std::borrow::Cow::Owned(image)
}

# LLP 2000.000: Snapback 3 — keep the core, flip two substrates, start at hub scale

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Draft
**Role:** Second opinion on LLP 2000; since 2026-09-12 also the design brief Snapback 4 builds (LLP 3000)
**Systems:** Snapback 3 product architecture, bounded computation, client data model, client API vocabulary, language and tooling, effects runtime, verification (simulation), performance budgets, process
**Author:** Claude Fable 5.1, at Charlie Cheever's request
**Date:** 2026-09-12
**Revised:** 2026-09-12 (adopted as the Snapback 4 design brief by LLP 3000 decision 3); 2026-09-12 (initial)
**Related:** [LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md) (parent), [LLP 3000](./3000-snapback4.explainer.md) (the Snapback 4 root that builds this brief), LLP 0000, [LLP 0329](./0329-agent-native-rebuild-diagnosis.research.md), [LLP 0329.014.004](./0329.014.004-bounded-data-programs-v1-design.rfc.md), [LLP 0329.016](./0329.016-agentic-dev-loop-from-scratch.rfc.md), [LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md), [LLP 1001](./1001-query-engine.spec.md), [LLP 1002](./1002-dev-loop.spec.md), [LLP 1012](./1012-local-first-v1.spec.md), [LLP 1018.003](./1018.003-truthful-and-composed-client.decision.md), [LLP 1018.008](./1018.008-elegance-after-the-corrections.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.007](./1018.008.007-affordable-offline-acquisition.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.008](./1018.008.008-locality-of-application-changes.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.009](./1018.008.009-residual-ergonomic-gaps.research.md), [LLP 1019](./1019-derived-delivery-custody.rfc.md), [LLP 1020](./1020-day-two-evolution-and-legible-ceilings.rfc.md), [LLP 1023](./1023-clique-builder-jump.rfc.md), [LLP 1024](./1024-single-lane-delivered-search.rfc.md), [LLP 1025](./1025-digest-final-emission-at-hub-scale.research.md), `rules/RULES.md`, `rules/NOT-DOING.md`, `benchmarks/runs/2026-09-05-clique-full-3x3/holistic-scores.md`

## 1. Summary

LLP 2000 is right that the whole application is the unit of design, right
that ownership comes before abstraction, and right to ask for deterministic
simulation and an early real deployment. Its weakness is that it treats the
two structural choices that produced Snapback 2's costs as open research
questions, and then proposes a first experiment at exactly the scale where
those costs are invisible. This document takes a position on both, names
what to keep from Snapback 2 verbatim, and rewrites the first slice so it
can fail for the right reasons.

The position, in one paragraph. Snapback 2's *core* is excellent and should
be copied: the rule language and its uniformity analysis, the structural-fact
family, one data language, typed states with no silence, refusals that paste
a rewrite, personas, the successor classifier, the derived per-recipient
log, the loop verbs and their sub-second budgets. Two *substrates* under
that core are where the record shows the hours went, and both should be
flipped rather than re-investigated: (1) the static certificate should keep
certifying **authorization and shape** and stop pricing **bytes and steps**,
which move to a deterministic runtime meter with honest `capped`/abort
outcomes; and (2) the client should hold **a replica of the viewer's declared
partition** — the local-first shape every shipped sync engine and Snapback
2's own `deliver` log converge on — instead of a per-query result cache
proven by receipts. Those two flips dissolve most of the machinery that
leaked into the application (receipts, coverage, halos, validators, grants,
ID slices, drains, `withHistory`, follower inboxes, per-table retention
words) and turn the 28-second acquisition into a bulk stream whose floor is
the device's write speed. The first slice should be built on the **hub
fixture** (1,000 memberships, 500 follows, 100,000 messages), with the
day-two change designed before the day-one screen, a native client in the
first slice, and a deterministic simulator as the primary test harness.

Three claims this document makes that LLP 2000 does not:

1. **The costs have two named causes, and both are decisions, not
   mysteries.** Byte-priced static certification and the receipt-proven
   result cache account for every ergonomic residual in LLP 1018.008.009
   except documentation. §2 shows the mapping.
2. **The first experiment must include the hub.** Snapback 2 was "elegant on
   day one and hostile on day two" (LLP 1020) and broke at 1,000 memberships
   (LLPs 1023, 1024, 1025). A two-principal slice will pass and teach nothing.
3. **The process is part of the product.** Seventy-one LLPs, over two
   thousand commits, and seven ceiling raises in fourteen days produced a
   sound system nobody can hold in their head. Snapback 3 needs one owner on
   the core, a simulator instead of a test matrix, and budgets anchored to
   competitors so that raising one means admitting a loss.

## 2. What the record says, read as causes

The evidence LLP 2000 §2 cites is right; its reading is too gentle. Every
row below is a Snapback 2 cost recorded in the corpus, with the substrate
that produced it. Numbers are the corpus's own and retain its scope limits.

| Recorded cost | Where | Cause |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Adding `editedAt: time ?` (44 bytes × 244 retained copies = 10,736) pushed `messages.searchAll` to 4,199,688 closure bytes against 4,194,304; the builder shrank a page from 20 to 19 | LLP 1018.008.008; 0.0.21 day-two diary | **Static byte pricing.** Closure bytes are a bound on what a receipt could ship; the product paid for the bound, not the work |
| The 1,000-membership digest fold certifies at 9,922,698 heap and 8,691,704 closure bytes and is refused; the recipient-keyed form is admitted at 2,733,253 / 1,848,418 | LLP 1025 §3 | **Static byte pricing** (heap = steps × 32 + closure scratch). A runtime meter would have run the fold and reported actual bytes |
| A message page is 50, not 200, because a row "prices at 16,016 bytes" and 200 exceed the 1 MiB output ceiling | LLP 1012 §3 | **Static byte pricing** of the worst-case row width |
| A per-recipient search projection is refused at 20,863,641 reserved steps against 1,000,000 | LLP 1024 §1 | **Static step pricing** of a derivation the engine would perform incrementally |
| Full acquisition of 10,008 messages: 67.4 s → 53.6 s → 32.6 s → 28.4 s against a 2–3 s objective; 201 requests, 200 acknowledgement mutations, 612 IndexedDB transactions; PowerSync reports 10,000 rows in 0.36 s native and 2.5 s on web | LLP 1018.008.007; LLP 1018.008.009 §4 | **Result cache, not replica.** Every 50-row page is a certified query, a receipt, a durable acknowledgement mutation, and a store swap |
| Adding a count to a thread makes the composite `partial` offline whenever count authority is missing, so the template declares an independent `history` query and `withHistory` bindings; 27 files / 1,718 lines → 28 / 1,751 after the fix | LLP 1018.008 F2; LLP 1018.008.009 §2 | **Result cache.** The count is not a fact the receipt covers, so the client cannot compose locally |
| `shared/thread.ts` describes table identity, row selection, order, create membership, draft fields and mutation targets a second time | LLP 1018.008 F2 | **Result cache.** Items must be reconstructed from answers because there is no local table to read |
| Sending stalled with 85 IDs remaining against a certified 352-ID slice on an 8,192-ID grant; recovery needed a "Sync now" action | LLP 1018.008 F1 | **Result cache + engine-minted IDs.** Prediction needs server ids before commit, so ids are granted in slices |
| A follower browser tab writes through a durable inbox row; the SDK carries `forwarding`, `writer-unresponsive`, `inbox-full`, `follower` states | LLP 1018.004 | **Result cache.** One writer per partition image; other tabs cannot mutate a receipt-proven image |
| Global search over 1,000 memberships costs ~1,010 HTTP requests through a client N-way merge | LLP 1024 §2 | **Result cache** (no local index over the partition) + **static pricing** (the server-side projection is refused) |
| The complete local runtime grew 7.8 KB → 89,843 gzip bytes; the ceiling was raised 34,000 → 50,000 → 66,000 → 82,000 → 82,500 → 84,000 → 85,000 → 90,000 | LLP 1000 §"Revised"; `rules/NOT-DOING.md` §Moves; LLP 1018.008 §10 | **Result cache.** The inputs list of `metrics/twin.jsonl` is coverage spans, extent facts, held directories, posting trees, fact integrity, swap journals — receipt machinery, not the interpreter |
| `Snapshot` carries `data, next, partial, receipt{state, source, seq, next, reason, rule, sites}, freshness{live, staleSince, connection}, local{predicted, retained, caughtUp, watermark}, refusal, status, reason` | LLP 1018.003 §1 | Both. Every field is a true fact about the mechanism; almost none is a fact about the application |
| `Schema::rule_hash` binds every queued write to the complete schema; an optional column on the subject table invalidates unrelated evidence | LLP 1018.002; LLP 1018.008.008 | **Result cache.** Evidence identity exists because held answers must be re-authorized; a replica re-runs the mutation at the server and needs no evidence identity |

Against that, what scored: on the 2026-09-05 three-scorer Clique run
Snapback 2 beat Convex on authorization (82 vs 79) and offline (81 vs 44)
and lost on queries and live (60 vs 85), client integration (59 vs 79),
setup and docs (55 vs 79), diagnostics and loop (66 vs 77), operations (53
vs 76) and scale (62 vs 80); overall 66 vs 81. The later single-lane 0.0.21
and 0.0.25 scores of 82 and 85 are real improvement and not a three-scorer
comparison. The wins are the core; the losses are composition, discovery,
scale and operations — the substrates and what sits on them.

LLP 2000 §2 summarises this as "costs arise where one layer must reconstruct
another layer's facts." True, and the layer doing the reconstructing is the
client, because it was designed as a cache that may not compute. Name the
cause and the remedy follows.

## 3. Where LLP 2000 is right, and should not be diluted

- **The whole application is the unit.** §3's four properties (composition,
  locality, continuity, performance) are the right acceptance vocabulary.
  Keep them as the headings of the scenario suite.
- **Ownership before abstraction.** §5's table is the best paragraph in the
  document. §8 below turns its fourth row into a fixed vocabulary.
- **Composed reads must not fabricate.** Unknown totals stay unknown; revoked
  rows do not linger. Both flips below preserve this; they change what is
  *known*, not what is *claimed*.
- **Writes have one lifecycle with durable admission distinct from commit
  and stable operation identity.** Keep LLP 1018.004's three outcomes.
- **Rust and SQLite.** Yes, and the engine's transaction, interpreter, rule
  evaluation and successor logic are the pieces to copy first (§5).
- **Prototype the experience before the core.** Yes — and §8 makes the
  prototype's API signatures binding on the implementation, not the reverse.
- **Deterministic simulation early**, citing TigerBeetle. This is the single
  most important sentence in LLP 2000 and §11 makes it the primary harness.
- **A real deployment and a native client early.** Yes; §11 puts the native
  client in slice one.
- **Parallel, fenced, 2000-series, promotion is the author's later call.** Yes.

## 4. Where LLP 2000 is wrong or leaves the wrong things open

### 4.1 It reopens the language and leaves the bound model open; invert that

§6 asks to "evaluate Quarry" and "evaluate any alternative against the same
contracts." The one-language decision took thirty-seven paper applications,
a three-family panel, a spelling spike and an author ruling (LLP
0329.014.004 §9, LLP 1004, LLP 1007 §4b) to close, and the shipped result is
the most readable artifact in Snapback 2: the chat schema is 129 lines and a
query is three to six. Reopening the language will consume the first month.
What *should* be reopened is the bound model under it, which §6 phrases as a
comparison to "concentrate early investigation on." Half of the Quarry
refusals a builder meets are the certifier's byte and step prices wearing
Quarry syntax; the `fn`/`proc` split and the page-to-list gap (LLP 1018.008
## Summary

Snapback 4 is one of two parallel candidates for the successor to Snapback 2,
and the sibling of Snapback 3, not its successor. It builds
[LLP 2000.000](./2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md) as
written: the Snapback 2 core kept verbatim, two substrates flipped up front —
resources metered at runtime instead of priced statically, and a client that
replicates the viewer's declared partition instead of caching receipted
results — proved first on the hub fixture, with a deterministic simulator as
the primary harness. Snapback 3 ([LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md), led by
Codex / GPT-6 Astra) keeps the same two questions open and discovers their
answers in its first slice. Snapback 2 ([LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md))
continues as the incumbent and the control. All three are judged by one
pre-registered comparison on one clock.

This document records the author's decisions of 2026-09-12 that created the
track, restates the bet on one page so a reader can orient without the brief,
fixes the bakeoff protocol, draws the fence, and plans the first four weeks.
The design argument and its evidence stay in LLP 2000.000. Only running code
will bind (`rules/RULES.md`).

## 1. Decisions (author, 2026-09-12)

On 2026-09-12 Charlie Cheever asked whether Claude Fable 5.1 should lead a
Snapback 4 while Codex / GPT-6 Astra leads Snapback 3 and Snapback 2
continues, "and see which one turns out best." The lead answered yes with
conditions; the author accepted them ("Sounds good") and asked for this
document. The decisions below are therefore the lead's proposal as accepted
by the author. Where the author has not separately ruled, the text says so.

1. **Three tracks, one comparison.** Snapback 2 continues as the incumbent
   and the control. Snapback 3 builds LLP 2000; Snapback 4 builds LLP
   2000.000. This is a bakeoff of *bets* — discover the substrates in the
   slice versus decide them first — not of models. The result will say more
   about the bets than about the leads, and §3 is written so that it can.
2. **The name is Snapback 4.** Siblings, not a sequence: "4" does not mean
   "after 3." A separate number gives the track its own corpus root,
   directory, package identity and data directory with no rename when the
   bakeoff resolves, and files never move in LLP. If Snapback 4 loses, it is
   a number that never shipped. "Snapback 3b" was considered and rejected:
   it reads as a variant of 3, and this is a different bet.
3. **Snapback 4 builds LLP 2000.000 as written.** Its §14 open questions are
   the lead's to decide inside the track, recorded in §7 as they are
   decided. The author rules on the bar, the comparison and promotion (§3).
4. **One owner on the core.** The lead writes the engine, the store and the
   client contract. Codex lanes build the fixture generator, the reference
   applications, the device witnesses and the periphery. Blind reviews from
   two model families are reserved for the two disclosure-sensitive
   mechanisms: partition inversion of read rules (LLP 2000.000 §7.1) and
   the meter's cut behaviour on non-uniform scans (LLP 2000.000 §6). This
   departs from the standing rule that Codex codes and Claude orchestrates,
   deliberately, for the core only.
5. **The clock is four weeks** from the first commit under `snapback4/` to
   the first slice running on the hub fixture. At the clock the author kills
   the track, merges its substrates into a survivor, or continues it. There
   is no third month without a ruling.
6. **Shared and not shared.** Shared with Snapback 3: the LLP corpus, the
   Clique harness and scorers, the fixtures, the briefs, and the simulator
   once either track has one. Not shared: code, except the Snapback 2 pieces
   both are expected to copy (LLP 2000.000 §5.1).
7. **Snapback 2 keeps shipping and freezes new substrate work for the
   duration.** Fix-forward, the loop bar, acquisition and papercuts continue;
   no new certificate axes, client custody mechanisms or ceiling changes.
   This is accepted here as a condition of the bakeoff. Making it binding on
   concurrent Snapback 2 sessions needs one line in `rules/NOT-DOING.md`'s
   Moves ledger, which this document does not write.
8. **Reading load.** This root, updated in place, and one append-only
   journal, `snapback4/JOURNAL.md`. Sub-LLPs only for a decision that must
   outlive the journal. No panels beyond decision 4's two reviews.

## 2. The bet on one page

**Keep verbatim from Snapback 2** (LLP 2000.000 §5.1): the rule language
with its uniformity and hoist analysis, `decided_true` and the
structural-fact refusals; one data language, Quarry, with `--` comments,
`require … else CODE`, `shape`, `merge`, `walk`, `chunk`; the value model
and its conformance corpus; typed read and write states with no silent state
and the refusal envelope `{code, family, site, message, rewrite, guide}`; the
successor classifier (widening additive, checked narrowing, exact projection
ownership); `deliver`'s engine-computed audience, tombstones and
`bootstrap`; `schedule`, `job`, `chunk` and rehearsal; personas; `init`,
`dev`, `check`, `doctor`, `run`, `test`, `why`, `trace`, `guide`; H0
`deploy`, `rollback`, `secret`; channels; assets; the word-prefix index.

**Flip 1 — certify authorization, meter resources** (LLP 2000.000 §6). The
certificate keeps every read site's shape, uniformity, completeness class
and prediction eligibility, and stops pricing bytes and steps. A
deterministic meter charges examined rows, index entries, rule probes,
steps, heap, output and rows written as the program runs, on the server and
in the client interpreter alike. A query cut by the meter returns `capped`
with no continuation past the cut, the same observable outcome as a static
ceiling cut today; a mutation aborts with `E_BOUND` naming actual work; a
job is chunked. `check --cost` prints the structural cost class; `test`
prints measured meters on the hub fixture.

**Flip 2 — the viewer's partition is the unit of sync** (LLP 2000.000 §7).
`sync to <audience> last N by <index>` on a table makes the engine maintain,
per principal, the rows that principal may read, as a change log with a
horizon; a table without the word is online-only, and `check` says which
rule term prevented inversion. The client holds a replica of its partitions,
one watermark each, a durable outbox with client-minted ids, and the
interpreter; queries run locally and are `complete` inside the horizon.
Revocation is a scope tombstone; `until-revoked` and `delivered-history`
survive as the client's leave policy. Two client tiers — online-only
without a store, local-first with one — share one language and one server.

**The vocabulary is fixed before the mechanism** (LLP 2000.000 §8):

```ts
type Read<T> = { data: T; complete: boolean; fresh: boolean; since?: number; next?: Cursor }
            | { loading: true } | { denied: Refusal };
row.pending: boolean;
type Write = { state: "pending" } | { state: "sent"; seq: number } | { state: "failed"; why: Refusal };
type Link = "online" | "offline" | "signed-out";
```

A screen that needs a word not on that card is a defect in the mechanism,
not a gap in the card.

**The hub comes first** (LLP 2000.000 §11): the Clique generator's `u0001`
with 1,000 memberships, `u0500` with 500 follows, and about 100,000
messages are the first fixture; day-two changes are designed before the
day-one screen; the native client is in slice one; physical floors are
measured before targets; the simulator is the primary harness.

#[test]
fn first_column_rename_refuses_a_conflicting_present_or_retained_destination(){
 let old="table items:\n  title: text <=40\n  name: text <=40\n  public\n  online only\n";
 let current="table items:\n  name: text <=40 renamed from title\n  public\n  online only\n";
 for dormant in [false,true] {for identical in [false,true] {
  let mut e=engine(old);seed(&mut e,"items",json!({"id":"a","title":"A","name":if identical {"A"}else{"B"}}));
  if dormant {adopt(&mut e,&old.replace("  name: text <=40\n",""),2).unwrap();}
  let generation=e.generation();let outcome=adopt(&mut e,current,3);
  if identical {outcome.unwrap();assert_eq!(field(&mut e,"items","a","name"),Value::String("A".into()));}
  else {let error=outcome.unwrap_err().to_string();assert!(error.contains("rename destination"),"{error}");assert_eq!(generation,e.generation());}
 }}

# Deploy: upgrade, real accounts and schema evolution

A release is immutable source over one durable store. JSON fields, bounded ready waits and worker roots: `guide release-json`. Deploy from the app directory:

```sh
bunx snapback4 check
bunx snapback4 test
bunx snapback4 deploy ./deployment
bunx snapback4 serve ./deployment --port 4400
```
For a complete real-user staging script and first-admin setup, run `guide staging`. Bulk account setup is restricted to fresh `deploy --staging` stores. Generations climb on every actual switch, including rollback and forward; selecting identical execution content is a no-op. `token --service ops --release ./deployment` works while serve runs. SDK guest/signup/login and proof seedAccounts retry explicit identity 429 refusals using retryAfterMs (Retry-After for older servers). Sign-in has at most four attempts within timeoutMs (default three minutes); signal cancels requests/waits. No lost or malformed reply is replayed. Time-gated mutations on the real clock need a bounded retry of the authored refusal (for example `NOT_OPEN`), with a deadline and short delay; never retry unrelated refusals. Development and classified staging proofs can advance the clock (`guide time`); production always uses wall time.

## Upgrade
`snapback4 upgrade [version]` resolves once and exactly pins core and installed Expo adapters with npm/bun/pnpm. Rebuild native apps after adapter changes; commit the lockfile, run check/typecheck/acceptance. `test` uses ./fixtures; `--fixture dir` overrides it. Use the password and first-admin recipes below.

## Deploy and password identity
Add `use identity password` (included below). Deploy from the app directory onto durable storage:
Serve admits bearer sessions; personas are 401 and /seed/dev doors 403, even through proxies. Signup creates an account, not a privileged role. See guide client for SDK session stores; auth/headless for account setup. HTTP auth returns session/denied; SDK signup/login from `snapback4/auth` returns ok/why. Check warns on installed version skew.
```sh
U=http://127.0.0.1:4400
curl -s "$U/auth/signup" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"email":"admin@example.test","password":"replace-with-a-private-password"}'
# {session:{principal,kind,token,expiresAt,scope}}
curl -s "$U/auth/login" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"email":"admin@example.test","password":"replace-with-a-private-password"}'
```
Keep the session and send `Authorization: Bearer <token>`. Wrong credentials or a disabled identity method are 401 E_AUTH. Sessions survive ordinary deployments and restarts until expiry/revocation; a replaced/reset store requires sign-in again. Never delete a store to make adoption pass. `serve --port N` accepts 0 or A-B; stdout's first line is its URL, stderr includes SNAPBACK4_READY. Wait for readiness. Layout: releases/N/ holds source; store.sqlite data/sessions/queues; assets/ uploads. The database owns current release/history; current is a text convenience copy, releases.jsonl a convenience ledger. Back up the whole directory, including sidecars/assets/releases, with serve and external workers stopped (CLIENT-AND-OPERATIONS.md §6). A restore does not rewind external effects.

## First admin: a service seat
Provision roles through an operator seat, with no public first-user race:
```quarry
use identity password
table admins:
  principal: principal
  unique principal
  immutable principal
  sync
  read <- .principal = viewer or viewer = 'service:ops'
  insert <- viewer = 'service:ops' or exists admins[principal = viewer]
mutation grantAdmin(principal: principal):
  return insert admins { principal }
table notes:
  owner: principal
  body: text <=200
  archived: bool default false
  status: enum('draft', 'live') default 'draft'
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
```
```sh
TOKEN=$(bunx snapback4 token --service ops --release ./deployment)
curl -s "$U/m/grantAdmin" -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN" \
  -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"args":{"principal":"user:SIGNED_UP_ID"}}'
unset TOKEN
```
This roster is synced. A whole service-seat branch may add the first member; ordinary callers must already hold the roster. Service grants add no device audience. The seat's own program lookups and `exists` obey read rules too: to inspect a roster, add `or viewer = 'service:ops'` to its read rule, as above. A rule probe has authorization visibility; a program probe does not. The mutation completes before the grant syncs; wait for the enrolled account's client to receive its scope before using offline staff reads. A synced row has at most one group-roster audience: cross-table crew grants are not a second group. Enroll those readers in the same roster or keep the table `online only` (`guide rule-recipes`). Mint locally against the same store. Token stdout is only the credential; prose is stderr. Protect it; it expires under the seat-session policy. Rotate the credential by revoking its session (guide services) and minting a replacement. Expired or revoked bearer sessions cannot invoke the mutation. A repeat grant hits uniqueness; add membership revocation deliberately. Programmatic account sessions use `mintSession(url, serviceToken, principal)` from `snapback4/worker` (guide services); service seats themselves are minted locally by `token`.

## Migrations and adoption
Optional/defaulted columns, new tables/ops, non-unique indexes and wider types are additive. Required new fields need a literal default or a declarative `backfill table name(fields: types): {column: expression}`; default now/viewer cannot invent historical values. Dev check/adoption refuses incompatible values with E_ADOPT. Release type narrowing instead retains each row whose present values cannot fit. Unique/cap conflicts still refuse atomically; repair active rows, or restore through a widened non-unique bridge, repair, then reinstate constraints. Prefer expand → migrate → contract for client compatibility.

## Rollback after widening, restore and prune
```sh
bunx snapback4 rollback ./deployment --to 1 --check
bunx snapback4 rollback ./deployment --to 1
```
`deploy ./deployment --check` previews deploy direction and restoration intent. `bunx snapback4 check --store ./deployment/store.sqlite --release 1` also previews a selection; `bunx snapback4 releases ./deployment latest` inspects it. Forward with `bunx snapback4 rollback ./deployment --to latest`. It refuses other serve flags. A timeout rejects cleanup rather than claiming the store is free.
Dev admits loopback personas or bearer sessions for queries, writes and the compatibility record; serve requires bearer sessions even for public rows. Identity config/sign-in endpoints are public. Seed/dev endpoints refuse in serve; system job doors require full service seats. `guide wire` gives the HTTP contract.
Public visitors obtain guest sessions with `use identity`; `use identity password` disables guests (`guide rule-recipes`).

Reference dependents also leave the active population. Rules, counts, maintained views and reacquired sync partitions see only active rows. Details: [SERVER.md — Release lifecycle](SERVER.md#release-lifecycle).

Check business compatibility separately: old payment code may accept payment while a new review row is dormant. Rollback preview identifies current safety guards/rules and read confidentiality the target loses, active table-population changes, and current → target quotas. Preserve the current domain, audience, guard or quota in a bridge release, or pause that workflow. Old source cannot supply absent product logic. An absent operation returns 404 `{denied:{code:"E_OP",…},generation:G}`; a mutation's incompatible arguments return 200 `{state:"failed",why:{code:"E_INPUT",…},generation:G}`.

## Bridge a safety gate before rollback
Use this beside the enum-widening rollback recipe above. Suppose v2 added `grounding.state = 'inspection'`; v1 holds only `'clear'`/`'hold'`. Selecting v1 hides that row and its children, so v1's `book` guard may allow a booking that v2 blocks. Counts and sample ids are snapshot dependencies, not predicate matches; later writes can change the plan.

1. Start from the old UI/operations, but keep v2's widened `grounding.state` domain, safety table, read audience and booking `require`. Save this complete source as `versions/bridge/schema.q` and deploy it with `deploy ./deployment --schema versions/bridge/schema.q --check`, then without `--check`.
2. While the gate still runs, migrate each inspection to a truthful old-domain hold through an authorized bridge mutation. Do not relabel it clear just to satisfy v1. Keep linked metadata until its parent can be safely represented; if v1 cannot invalidate newer metadata, keep that behavior in the bridge too.
3. Prove the same booking/payment still refuses for the blocked case and that the named lists/counts preserve the intended business denominator. Re-run the final old-source preview against the repaired live population.
4. Select v1 only when those checks pass. If the old source cannot preserve the invariant, keep the bridge or pause that workflow. Retained storage cannot supply absent logic.

## What the old release sees
| Request after rollback | Result |
|---|---|
| An operation absent from the old release | HTTP 404 `{denied:{code:"E_OP"}}` |
| Bare point query of a dormant row | Reads null |
| Point query or mutation with an explicit `! else CODE` guard | Refuses its guard code (`NOT_FOUND` when authored that way) |
| Added columns after rollback | Ignored by the older release; their cells stay dormant |
| Counts, rules, maintained views and sync | Only the active population |
| Compatible forward to the newer release | Original dormant values restored |

This proof assumes v1 declares `readItem(item)` returning `items[item]` and `editItem(item)` with `row = items[item]! else NOT_FOUND`; v2 adds `newOnly` and writes an enum value v1 cannot hold. Keep the bearer session in `headers`:
```js
await cli('rollback', deployment, '--to', '1'); const absent = await fetch(url + '/q/newOnly', {method:'POST', headers, body:'{"args":{}}'});
assert.equal(absent.status, 404); assert.equal((await absent.json()).denied.code, 'E_OP');
assert.equal((await post('/m/editItem', {item})).why.code, 'NOT_FOUND');
assert.equal((await post('/q/readItem', {item})).data, null);
await cli('rollback', deployment, '--to', 'latest'); assert.equal((await post('/q/readItem', {item})).data.status, 'new');
```
Rollback previews lead with records hidden from tables the target release has, then empty/defaulted legacy cells and newer-only table rows. Counts precede read rules; they do not infer birth release or a viewer’s query result. Declare a reverse projection for a truthful old representation, or keep a bridge. Guard/refusal and query/count/safety diffs follow. Verify filtered/paged/per-viewer results and denominators. Literal-default backfills print `table.column ← value on N rows` in previews and cold/hot deploy receipts: interpreted historical fields, not physical writes. Preview shows old and new rule clauses and mutation `require`s side by side, preserving Boolean parentheses. A proved tightening is a note, including `require A else X` becoming `require A else X; require B else Y` when A's refusal contract is unchanged; changed codes/messages still warn. A loosening or unproved implication is a WARNING, including rollback that removes a new guard. Unchanged clauses and consulted keys, fields, rules, auth and visibility are silent. Unread column domains and forward domain widening do not unprove a probe; narrowing still gets exact dormant-value/population warnings. Changed dependencies group once: verify refusal cases. Comparison never changes admission. Missing operations/schedules, populated removed tables, changed clauses and job bodies are named separately. An unchanged job compiling differently after a dependency change gets a note. Predicate-only read-through changes preserve bound captures and parent lifetimes; structural capture changes and new uncaptured rows still need safe audience correspondence. Receipts and previews count storage by kind: `1 row, 2 cells, 0 index entries dormant`. Dormant rows cover their retained cells; standalone cell counts/samples belong only to active rows. Bytes/assets follow the same disjoint ownership. Samples are labelled and unique within each kind/path, for example `rows notes [A]; cells notes.color [B, C]`. A retained index definition represents logical entries to rebuild, not a dormant physical index. A widening note describes values later writes could use; a compatibility WARNING counts only current values the selected release cannot hold. Compatible forward has no such warning.

## Clients across rollback
Save versions/v1/schema.q (and any other .q modules with their original names) outside snapback/ and create `snapback/client-compatibility.json` containing `{"baseline":"versions/v1","since_release":2}`. `check`/`dev` emit added row/result fields as optional, labelled "Since release 2", retaining older result value domains. Required operation parameters remain required; only declared optional, defaulted or deprecated arguments may be omitted. Baseline generation also warns about old-client input breaks and result/refusal changes. This is your release label, not server generation; ordinary types stay required without opt-in. Use `row.color ?? "none"` for the baseline fallback. Keep contemporary fixtures in versions/v1/fixtures/; deploy with `--schema versions/v1` and test with `--source versions/v1` (guide testing). Baselines resolve from the original project root, including saved-source deploys.

`useRelease()` from snapback4/react follows the authenticated catalog even when query gates are closed: loading, denied, or `{generation, operations, tables}` with public argument/field shapes. Generated `api.op.available(release)` is false while unready or when name/kind/existing-argument signatures differ or the server adds a required argument; extra optional served parameters are compatible. This checks capability, not authorization or unchanged business semantics; still handle E_OP/E_INPUT and authored guard refusals when a switch races it. The SDK withdraws old metadata on observed generations, polls while subscribed, and returns loading offline/on catalog failure; account retirement/close withdraws it. Keep draft state above generation-sensitive read/action gates; remount only the gated children. Dormant means unavailable on this release, not deleted. Test v2 → v1 → v2 with one frontend: field fallbacks, missing operations, retained drafts and restored values. Alternatively check selected immutable source in a separate copy and rebuild its matching frontend. Preserve deployment source; Expo/Exact runtime changes also require a device rebuild.

1. **Writes erase native fingerprints for hidden historical inputs.** [backfills.rs:944](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b21-evol/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store/backfills.rs:944) hashes only the active projection, treating retained hidden inputs as null.

   Reproduced: create a native v2 row `{input:1, output:99}` under `{output:input * 10}`; carry the declaration into v3 while dropping `input`; execute even a no-op `{output:99}` update; select v1, then v2. Forward adoption refuses with “recomputation differs.” The unchanged retained input remains `1`, but the write replaced its accepted fingerprint with null. This breaks §8’s preservation of authored native outputs.

2. **Recompute agreement still refuses fully reversed outputs.** [backfills.rs:648](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b21-evol/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/store/backfills.rs:648) rejects before the new agreement exception at line 654, which applies only to still-transformed outputs.

   Reproduced: v1 has `input:int, output:int`, seeded `{input:1, output:0}`. Adopt v2 declaring `{output:input * 10}`, roll back to v1, then write `{input:2, output:20}`. Re-entering v2 refuses with “output changed while backfill … was absent,” although recomputation produces exactly `20`. This omits the fully reversed case from §8’s agreement rule.
